# Gameplay Coverage

Every retained feature traced from where the player meets it to what it changes and who reads the change: entry, preconditions and state read, the calculation and the state changed, the downstream consumer, what persists, how it is explained, and what tests it. Audited on 2026-10-10 against `43d10f8b`, and its open items revisited after `9ae9879b` (`docs/verification/open-items.json` holds each one’s disposition, recorded before the work, and its outcome). Written by `node tools/coverage.mjs` from `docs/verification/audit/*.json`.

Audited again on 2026-10-12, after the connected-journey pass, and every row that pass changed was brought up to date (Corrected On 2026-10-12, below). Four reviews of that audit the same day found it said more than was done, and every row they named was settled by the standard below; the rows read by eye against it are listed under Read By Eye, and the Totals count the verified rows read apart from those called verified and not read. The third found 36 commands that came in after the audit and its revisit (from `f899ecd7`, 2026-10-10, and later) in no row, while this page said every retained feature was traced; each is traced and read now (Traced On 2026-10-12, below). The fourth found rows verified on the change alone, or on a reader the clock calls run by the test in its place: a medical debt’s report a year on, a tax plan revised or caught up and never played on, a term’s maturity after its interest is taken, a stopped debit card’s purchases, a dismissed decision’s refusal, savings linked behind the current account and never reached by a card’s autopay or a loan, and an overdraft’s fee once opted into; each is partial now and says what isn’t checked. Checking what the fourth settled, the same day, found more verified on a reader no test reaches through the command: an elective shift’s payday and the wages it records for the return, a kept plan’s hold on sleep and Look After, a treatment’s later months, tolls and emergencies, what a doctor chosen decides, a meal’s hunger toll, and a decision acted on refused a second time; each is partial now too. Every row is checked by `tools/coverage.mjs`, which writes this page and fails when one doesn’t hold: each test it names by its title is in the file it names, each line it or its Note cites falls inside a test, every line of every range, a row called verified names a test in the gate, and a command called verified names a test file that issues it, its action too. It fails as well where this page and `docs/capabilities.json` disagree on a command: every command a live capability names is in a row here or in the connected-journey trace (148 are here); a capability called Verified has every row of its commands verified and read; a command not verified here isn’t under a capability whose verified layer says Verified, and the capability’s limits name each of its rows not verified, by the row’s own name (life.look_after:wash for one of its actions), in a sentence that gives its word here, partial, or unresolved (or out of reach) where only a fixture reaches it; and a command reached only by a fixture sits in a capability called Partial. Until the fourth review it held only the first, a Verified capability’s rows verified, and the last, so a capability called Implemented or Partial could be silent on a command this page calls partial, or keep Verified in its verified layer; and until its settlement was checked a sentence naming a command named every action of it, so limits that called one action of life.look_after partial passed for three more. Whether the rest of what the limits say of a command agrees with its row (that they don’t call checked what the row calls partial) is held by reading. Whether a test checks what reads the change is held only by reading: until the first review this page said every test a row names was read for it, until the second the rows read were named in a sentence somebody typed, and until the third the Totals counted every row called verified as verified, read or not. What the pass added is traced in `docs/verification/connected-journey/TRACE.md`, not here: a piece judged in its segments, the scene together with a partner or a company, the workbook’s commands, a brief’s reader, the director’s craft notes, the actor’s work so far, and the guide’s reference.

**Verified**: traced end to end, and a named test in the gate (`tests/*.test.ts`) checks what the row says reads the change (its Downstream), driving the change through the engine, by a command or the clock. A test that sets the change or its reader’s input by hand, or calls a function in place of the command or the clock, checks less: the row says so, and where what reads the change is checked only that way, or not at all, it’s partial. A reader the clock calls on its own (the day’s draw, the idle check, a rehearsal kept) called by the test in its place, or given its draw, is one of those. A precondition set by hand (wages typed in, a document put in) is setup, where the command and what reads it still run through the engine. A reader called after the command, on the state the command left, is checked by that call: its figure read (cardRoom after card.lock), or a purchase made through the engine’s own spend, the way every day’s shopping is made, where spend is what reads the change. What checks less is the same reader called on state set by hand, given its draw, or run in place of the clock that runs it: a function called in place of the command or the clock. Until the fourth review this page counted those two as setup without drawing that line, and `docs/capabilities.json` said any function called directly checked less, so a row could pass one standard and owe a word under the other. On a life no new one lives, the row says that too where the place decides what’s tested (a bank’s terms, a state’s or a country’s rule, a published wait). The rows the third review read say it; until then most bank rows ran on New York, Icelandic or London lives and didn’t. A reader that only shows (a suggestion, a look) is under Explained, not Downstream. A trait or a knob is the input itself, so its row is checked by twins: a test that varies it alone and checks the reader the row names. A Home or Profile row is a panel, and what it shows and what its controls do are what it’s for: it’s verified where a test renders it and checks that, the row naming what the test doesn’t read, and a test of the engine function a panel calls checks the figure, not the panel. `tools/acceptance-ui.mjs` plays the built app outside the gate: it can support a row and never makes one verified alone. Until 2026-10-12 a test that exercised the command was taken for that, and rows were verified on a test that checked the change and not its reader, on a fixture no new life reaches, or on no test; they’re corrected below. A row called verified and not read is verified only as the audit of 2026-10-10 and the open-items revisit left it: its Status says so, and the Totals don’t count it as verified. Mutation-tested only where a repair’s commit says so; the rest were traced, not mutated. **Partial**: works, with a named gap (untested, tested only by hand or as a function, or a consumer missing). **Unresolved**: declared or reachable only through systems the owner removed, or reached only by a fixture (a place no new life lives, such as a bank, a card or a country’s rule no New Haven life has), reported rather than deleted, each with its recommendation where the owner decides. **Legacy**: a compatibility path for a save from before 2026-10-09, tested through legacy state; no new life reaches it. **Informational**: said back to the player and meant to decide nothing (a name, a portrait).

**Read by eye against the standard:** 56 of the 190 rows called verified (29%), and 158 of all 328 rows. Those 56 are the verified rows. The other 134 are called verified as the audit of 2026-10-10 and the open-items revisit left them, with what a script can hold held (each citation lands, a verified row names a test in the gate, a verified command names a test file that issues it), and whether their tests check what reads the change wasn’t read, so they’re counted apart, as the audit’s word, not as verified. Of the 125 rows that were verified when they were read, 93 (74%) failed the standard. They were read for being named by a review or because a capability `docs/capabilities.json` called Verified names their command, not drawn at random, so that isn’t a measure of the rest, but it’s reason to hold none of the rest as verified until it’s read.

## Traced On 2026-10-12

36 commands that `docs/capabilities.json` names came in after the audit of 2026-10-10 and its revisit, and were in no row until the third review of 2026-10-12 found them: a leave and its way back, a repeated year and an Incomplete, the Yale account and the aid office, a state’s tax and the payment plans, the liens, the Social Security number, a second bank, and a switch’s last steps. Each was traced that day and read against the standard, and stands at 24 verified, 7 partial and 5 unresolved, reached only by a fixture; 3 of the partial were verified until the fourth review read them again. Its row is in its area below, with the rest.

| Area | Item | Status | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| School | school.request_leave | verified | Through the command and the clock, a medical leave is decided, withdraws the term’s courses to be taken again, sets the program back, ends classes and keeps Yale Health as Affiliate cover, and extends an F-1’s I-20 (a London-born student); a personal one begun in the spring returns the term’s aid on the rebate schedule and lets the cover lapse; no notice by its date dismisses the student. The illness a medical leave asks for is put in by hand. |
| School | school.cancel_leave | verified | Through the command, asked and called off with confirmation: the decision never comes, the leave is on the record as canceled, and the program goes on at its terms (offset 0, both terms enrolled) through the clock. |
| School | school.appeal_leave | verified | Through the command and the clock, an appeal from a student cleared early in the term rescinds the leave, restores the term’s courses and classes count again; cleared too late, or not cleared, it’s upheld; a second appeal is refused. The crisis that brings Yale Health’s assessment is put in by hand. |
| School | school.return_notice | verified | Through the command and the clock, notice is refused without a physician’s clearance after a medical leave, given once, and the student is back at the return term with the courses left; without it the leave ends in dismissal. |
| School | school.reregister | verified | Through the command, a student back from a leave registers once, and the fall back’s courses are attended through the clock. No test refuses it for a hold. |
| School | school.buy_affiliate | verified | Through the command, Affiliate cover is bought for both terms less the fee paid, Yale Health covers back to the lapse, and a bill had in it is brought down. |
| School | school.withdraw | verified | Through the command, withdrawal ends the place and a leave with it, calls off the next term, and by the fifteenth day of classes cancels Yale Health back to the term’s start, bills the care had in it again and refunds the premium; later, cover runs thirty days. |
| School | school.make_up | verified | Through the command, an Incomplete left by an illness is made up session by session and passed, and one open at Commencement confers the held degree once made up. |
| School | school.extend_incomplete | verified | Through the command, the month runs a month more, a second asking is refused, and past the old date the course is still Incomplete through the clock. |
| School | school.request_repeat | verified | Through the command and the clock, a year failed is repeated: the program set back two terms, the year’s courses meeting again, the first attempts kept; a last year’s is put back on the calendar and billed past six. The degree reading the best attempt is checked on attempts set by hand. |
| School | school.cancel_repeat | partial | Pressed on the rendered panel, through the engine: refused without confirmation with its warning, and canceled. No test checks what follows: that the chair’s decision doesn’t come, and the degree withheld at the date when nothing else is asked. |
| School | school.request_extra_term | verified | Through the command and the clock, an additional term is granted for the course unpassed, only it meets, it’s billed past six and confers in December. The sixth term’s evaluation and the retake’s pass are set by hand. |
| School | school.pay_balance | verified | Through the command, a term billed past six is paid in parts, the hold lifting on the last, and a diploma held for the account is conferred once it’s paid. A balance comes only from a term past the sixth. |
| School | school.request_aid_review | verified | Through the command and the clock, the aid office excepts tuition where a medical leave made the extra terms, and the term then begun bills nothing for it; without one, it bills tuition. Asked twice, refused. |
| School | school.request_conversion | verified | Through the command and the clock, an assigned shift called in sick is converted, paid in January at the work-study rate as a scholarship and on the year’s earnings record the return reads; refused to a student with no aid and a second time. |
| Money | bank.open_current | verified | Through the command and the clock, Capital One opens at nothing and its funding arrives in its days, Bank of America reviews for two business days and sends a refused deposit back, a second at the same bank is refused, an F-1 is refused by Capital One and opens at Wells Fargo in a branch. |
| Money | bank.set_primary | verified | Through the command, the primary moves what it says and nothing set before: wages and the scholarship stay with the account they’re paid into, a payment by hand comes from the primary, and a card from another bank reads the new primary’s. |
| Money | bank.redirect_deposits | verified | Through the command and the clock, pay goes on to the old account until the lead has run and to the new one after, and Yale’s pay follows after its cycle. |
| Money | bank.set_order_source | partial | Through the command, the order’s source moves and closing the old account is no longer refused for it (linkedTo). No test plays the order’s day after the move and checks it paid from the new account. |
| Money | card.set_autopay_from | verified | Through the command and the clock, autopay pays from the account named, held for the card bank’s days where it’s another bank’s and not where it’s its own, and the alert warns of that account’s shortfall; a closing account is refused. |
| Money | bank.set_loan_repayment | partial | No test issues it: moving a loan’s repayments to another account is code with nothing that checks it. Closing an account a loan repays from is refused (bank-lifecycle.test.ts), on the account the borrowing set. |
| Money | bank.close_current | verified | Through the command and the clock, closing is refused for each thing still on the account, then closes it with nothing lost, the remainder arriving in its days and the history kept; a pending card payment holds it until it posts. |
| Money | bank.cancel_switch | unresolved | Reached only by a fixture: the US has no switching service, so a New Haven life is refused a switch (bank.switch) and never has one to call off. On a British life, called off before its window nothing moves, and past its window it’s refused. As bank.switch, the owner decides. |
| Money | bank.stop_balance_transfer | unresolved | Reached only by a fixture: on a British life, the balance transfer alone is stopped and the switch moves the payments, the old account kept open with its money. No New Haven life has a switch. As bank.switch, the owner decides. |
| Papers | tax.pay_state | verified | Through the command, Connecticut’s balance is paid penalty first on a New Haven life, a state is refused more than it’s owed, and a lien paid off is marked paid. The year’s wages or its return are put in by hand. |
| Papers | tax.amend | verified | Through the command and the clock, on New York and California lives no new one lives, an amendment is processed in ten weeks, each authority’s part owed or refunded, and joins or defaults a plan. The record’s change is a corrected W-2 set by hand. No test amends a Connecticut part, the only state a student files. |
| Papers | tax.apply_plan | verified | Through the command and the clock, a monthly plan is approved, collection stops and the penalty’s rate halves, its payments are taken, a missed one is caught up or ends it and collection resumes; a short plan has no fee and is paid by its days; on a New Haven life it covers the IRS’s balance alone. |
| Papers | tax.revise_plan | partial (verified until 2026-10-12) | Through the command, a plan an amendment’s tax put in default is revised for the IRS’s fee and is active again (finance-paths.test.ts:195-197, a New York life); its other call, in defects-g8-8.test.ts, checks a refusal. No test plays a payment at the revised amount, or runs the clock past the default’s date to show the revision kept the plan from ending, the two readers the row names. |
| Papers | tax.catch_up_plan | partial (verified until 2026-10-12) | Through the command, a missed payment is caught up, the plan is active again, and the ledger balances (finance-paths.test.ts:159-167, a New York life); its twin left behind ends after CP523’s thirty days and collection resumes (finance-paths.test.ts:170-181). The caught-up plan isn’t played past the date the default would have ended it, so what reads the catch-up (TAX_PLAN_DEFAULT_KIND, its notice canceled) isn’t checked: the twin shows what happens without it, not that the plan is spared. |
| Papers | tax.apply_state_plan | verified | Through the command and the clock, a state’s plan is decided, paid, missed and caught up or ended, a lien dropped or filed by its rules, on New York, California, New Mexico, Pennsylvania, Louisiana, District and Hawaii lives, none a life a new one lives. No test asks for Connecticut’s plan, the only one a student’s life files; its fee, decision time and missed-payment rule weren’t found and are SLATE’s. |
| Papers | tax.catch_up_state_plan | partial (verified until 2026-10-12) | Through the command, New York’s missed payment is made up and the plan is active (state-tax.test.ts:126-130, a New York life no new one lives). The test then empties the bank and plays two months, and the plan ends on the next payment missed (state-tax.test.ts:131-135), as it would have had the catch-up failed, so it can’t tell one that worked from one that didn’t: what reads the catch-up (STATE_PLAN_END_KIND, canceled by it) isn’t checked. No test catches up Connecticut’s, the plan a student’s life files for. |
| Papers | tax.lien_release | verified | Through the command and the clock, Connecticut’s lien, filed by the calendar and paid, is released only on request, once, after its days; Hawaii’s asks its $25. The return owing is put on by hand. |
| Papers | tax.lien_dispute | unresolved | Reached only by a fixture: California’s lien on a non-filer’s assessment, set by calling the engine’s functions, is withdrawn through the command with its $10 back, and one that secured tax owed is refused. A student files only in Connecticut, and no test withdraws Connecticut’s lien as filed in error. Owner recommendation: keep it for the place a life might file, or test Connecticut’s. |
| Papers | tax.federal_lien_withdraw | unresolved | Reached only by a fixture: the IRS files its notice only where $10,000 or more is unpaid at the final notice (FEDERAL_LIEN.threshold), past anything work-study and a stipend leave owing; tested on a New Yorker given $150,000 of wages by hand, refused before its release and withdrawn after. No test has one filed in error released. Owner recommendation, as the other liens past a student’s reach. |
| Papers | tax.request_waiver | unresolved | Reached only by a fixture: Oregon’s penalty, set by calling the engine’s functions, is waived through the command at 25% after an assessment; 70% and 50% aren’t tested. Every life files in Connecticut. Owner recommendation, as the other liens past a student’s reach. |
| Papers | documents.apply_ssn | partial | Through the command: refused before registering and a second time, the card three weeks on and there across a reload, a citizen refused; the ID card refused until it comes and issued after. Not checked: a bank’s branch hour once one comes, a lender refusing for want of one, the W-7’s seven weeks more, and the plan by mail. |

## What Moved, And Why

23 items moved status when the open items were revisited. A row says what moved it: built or connected, tested, or reclassified. A reclassification isn’t new functionality.

| Area | Item | Was | Now | Why |

| Life, Health And Time | calendar.skip | partial | verified | Tested: Look After refused while a plan is due, allowed once skipped; a second skip refused; across a reload. |

| Life, Health And Time | health.prepay | partial | unresolved | Works and is tested, but only as a London fixture: a new life is the New Haven student, who is refused it. Reclassified from partial for that reason, not for any change. |

Shown on the partner line. |

## Corrected On 2026-10-12

160 rows were corrected when the matrix was audited again on 2026-10-12 and reviewed four times, 102 of them from verified: 96 to partial, and 6 to unresolved, reached only by a fixture. Each said more than its test checked, was verified on a fixture no new life reaches, named a test by a name it no longer has or none that checks it, described what the code no longer does, or didn’t say its tests ran on a life no new one lives where the place decides what they test. A row a review found moved the wrong way is back where its test puts it, and says so. A row says what it said and what it says now, with the evidence; the same words are in its Note below. Nothing here is new functionality: where the code changed, the row says when and why.

| Area | Item | Status | Said | Says Now |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Life, Health And Time | life.sleep | partial (verified until 2026-10-12) | Note: hours not validated as finite (the clamp passed NaN), and a start with no upper bound. Verified, while no cited test slept through a commitment. | From 2026-10-10 a night asks a number of hours and a start within the day ahead (tests/life-fixes.test.ts). Through the command, a night moves the clock by its hours, restores energy and pays down the debt, and is slept once whatever midnight it crosses (simulation.test.ts). No test sleeps through a commitment with life.sleep and checks what reads it: present() or the PLAN_KIND handler marking it missed, or the notice. The therapy session slept through (life-fixes.test.ts) sets awakeSince by hand, and plans.test.ts misses a plan through advanceTo. No inHospital check. |

The switch itself is reached only by a fixture, Iceland’s and Britain’s services (lending.test.ts, payments.test.ts). cash.test.ts:134-149 (Icelandic lives; the purchases made through spend); accountlife.test.ts:210-224, 237-259 (linked through the command at RBC and Deutsche Bank, on Toronto and Berlin lives); tests/defects-r3b-1.test.ts ‘offers the link, says what it covers, turns it On, and On pays the loan from Chase savings’, ‘is paid from Chase savings when the Chase current account can’t...’ (a card’s autopay through the clock), ‘covers nothing once the loan is repaid from Citi, and says so’; tests/defects-r6-1.test.ts ‘isn’t offered Off, where pressing it would be refused, and comes back with savings at the primary bank’ | partial (verified until 2026-10-12) | Through the command, the day’s shopping made through spend goes on the credit card, or comes out of the wallet first, as chosen (payments.test.ts:78-86, cash.test.ts:134-149), and savings linked pay what the current account can’t, and only where linked (payments.test.ts:51-67), on Icelandic lives; linked at RBC and Deutsche Bank, a bill paid from savings is charged its withdrawal fee and held to Germany’s allowance (accountlife.test.ts:210-224, 237-259). From 2026-10-12 the link draws, for every payment, on easy-access savings at the bank of the current account the payment comes from: the primary for the day’s spending, the account a card’s autopay is set on for that autopay, and a loan’s repayment account for the loan; a change of primary leaves a card’s autopay and a loan’s repayment where they were. How You Pay lists each paying account and what it pays, and turning the link On is refused only where none has such savings. With Chase savings, a Citi loan repaid from Chase and Citi made primary, the link is offered, turned On, and pays the loan off from Chase savings (bank.repay_loan), and a card’s autopay left on Chase is paid from Chase savings through the clock where until then it bounced (defects-r3b-1.test.ts). Until then this row read ‘savings existence’, and said the card’s autopay reaching linked savings wasn’t checked. What isn’t: a loan’s monthly payment drawn from them by the clock (lending.test.ts sets the link by hand and doesn’t check which account paid; defects-r3b-1.test.ts pays the loan off by the command). The fourth review didn’t name this row; it was read while settling the rows it did. |

| bank.switch | command | BankLending.tsx:323 | banksFor, chex, collections, photo ID, overdraft, SWITCHING | schedules bank.switch_complete on the service’s working days where the country has a switching service; refused with the steps to take where it hasn’t (from 2026-10-10) | new current/savings receive balances; standing orders/scheduled payments re-pointed; overdraft limit dropped; fees/interest by new bank's product | accounts | switch panel, refusal reasons | lending.test.ts ‘runs Iceland’s switching service in its twelve business days, and moves every balance whole’, ‘runs no switch where there’s no service, and says what to do instead’; payments.test.ts ‘moves with the accounts when a switching service moves the bank’; arrears.test.ts:159 | unresolved (verified until 2026-10-12) | Named, not numbered. Since 2026-10-10 a switch runs only where the country has a switching service, on its working days; elsewhere it’s refused with the steps to take, where until then it opened, moved and closed everything the same day. The US has none, and every life banks in New Haven, so the New Haven student meets only the refusal (lending.test.ts ‘runs no switch where there’s no service...’, through the command). The switch itself is reached only by a fixture, Iceland’s and Britain’s services (lending.test.ts, payments.test.ts). Unresolved, as health.prepay is: kept as written until the owner says otherwise, since reaching it needs a life lived outside the school, which the owner’s brief of 2026-10-09 held back. |

In the gate only Study It is pressed and read on the rendered Profile: the panel’s search and its filters, Open Its Brief and Tape It in the pane it opens are played only by tools/acceptance-ui.mjs, outside the gate, and the genre to study’s words are tested on nextGenre, a function, not on the panel. Until later on 2026-10-12 this Note didn’t say so. Since then the paragraph that holds them, You love:, is rendered from the Profile, and its loved genres are checked to open once the guide’s chapter that opens a genre is read, and not before (defects-r8-1.test.ts); the sentence’s own words aren’t checked there. |
